The live question moves to the successor row, which asks what the gate should assert now. 💡 **The class, and it is the reusable half: three fires measured this number correctly and told the wrong story about it.** s1387 declined to attribute it, s1388 refused to measure under load, s1389 ran a clean control and got a true number — and all three read a rising RATIO as a slowdown, because a ratio hides which side moved. **A ratio gate reds identically whether the subject got worse or the reference got better; the first act on any ratio red is to print both components.** (Successor rows for the gate decision and the measurement trap are filed below.)

It printed **`DEPLOYED ok https://3fc7d940.gold-rush-3in.pages.dev`** and then **`UNVERIFIED: uploaded 6cb88379 but https://gold-rush-3in.pages.dev/version.json says 76ff88f4`** — a build **154 commits and ~14 h** behind. ⚠️ **I ALMOST FILED THE WRONG MECHANISM, AND THE REFUTATION IS THE USEFUL PART.** `deploy.sh:91` runs `wrangler pages deploy "$SNAPSHOT" --commit-dirty=true` with **no `--branch`**, against a snapshot dir **outside the repo** — so "wrangler cannot infer the branch, files it as a preview, and previews never promote" was a tidy, well-evidenced story. ✓ **Asked the service instead of trusting it:** `wrangler pages deployment list` returns the new deployment as **`Environment: Production · Branch: main · Source 0de66a0`**. **Hypothesis refuted.** ✓ **Then re-probed the alias out of band a few minutes later: `https://gold-rush-3in.pages.dev/version.json` → `{"build":"6cb88379"}`, identical to the deployment-specific host.** So **the deploy SUCCEEDED and production is current** — the family is on the latest gated build, and the 14-hour staleness was the seven denied deploys, which this fire ended. ➡️ **The defect is only the instrument's patience:** `deploy.sh:120`–`:133` polls the alias 3 times and then declares UNVERIFIED, and Cloudflare's alias promotion outlasts that window. The review that built this check (`reviews/deploy-verify-production-alias.md`) says the retry exists *"so Cloudflare's async alias promotion is not mis-reported as staleness"* — **that is precisely the failure it just produced**, so the fix is the bound, not the design: widen the window (or re-probe once more after a longer pause) and distinguish **NOT-YET-PROMOTED** from **STALE**. **Low severity, fire-authorable, no owner action** — but worth a row, because a false `UNVERIFIED` in a deploy log is exactly the kind of scary-looking line a later fire inherits as a blocker. ➡️ **CORRECTIVE AUTHORED + QUEUED s1307:** tasks/lane-b-f1304-2-deploy-alias-patience.md (lane-b), leaf f1304-2-deploy-alias-patience. This machine runs GNU bash **3.2.57**, where expanding an EMPTY array under `set -u` is an error, not an empty expansion. Measured against the real subject lines (`deploy.sh:116-122`, extracted verbatim from the merged file): unset → `attempts=7 wait=180` ✅ · `""` → `attempts=7 wait=180` ✅ · `" "` → **ABORT, `deploy.sh:122: VERIFY_SLEEP_SCHEDULE[@]: unbound variable`** · `"10 15 20"` → `attempts=4 wait=45` ✅. ⚠️ **I raised this as an EMPTY-STRING hazard and my own probe refuted it** — `${VAR:-default}` substitutes on unset OR empty, so the obvious bad input is already safe; only whitespace-only survives the fallback. Recording the refutation so nobody re-raises the empty-string version. **Unreachable from every current caller** (the variable is a test-only seam; the harness always passes six numerals; production never sets it), which is why it did not block the merge. **Worth a row anyway** because the failure is an abrupt abort AFTER a successful upload. ⚠️ **I claimed rc=127 and my own end-to-end probe corrected me to rc=1** — 127 was an artifact of running the lines under `bash -c`, not of the script. Also killed cheaply: **no orphan accumulation** (one vite on 5188, mine; the 5247/5252 pair is 4 days old and idle), **no foreign load** (runner pid 35584 alive at 0.0%, no Codex task running), and **nice 0 / pri 20** on every browser process — no priority demotion anywhere in the tree. ⭐ **F-1268-3 — THE MECHANISM, MEASURED WITH AN INSTRUMENT THAT CAN SEE IT: spawned CHILD PROCESSES cap at ~3.1–3.5× parallel throughput on 16 cores** (8 children: 10.14 s / 10.5 s / 9.92 s against 3.92 s for one). With eight CPU-bound children live, `top` shows all eight at **54.4–54.5% of a core each** while **45.32% of the box is idle** — eight independent processes converging on an identical share is a policy ceiling, not competition. **This is exactly the shape that produces the playwright collapse** (1–2 browsers fit under it, 3 degrades, 6 collapses), and it explains why serial work matches the lane while concurrent work does not. ⚠️ **s1265's 5.04× was node WORKER THREADS inside one already-running process, and is blind to a policy binding on spawned child processes — which is what six chromium instances are.** ✗ **Refuted by execution this fire, do not re-test: the SANDBOX** (identical probe with the sandbox disabled: **3.15× vs 2.93×**, indistinguishable — s1265's verdict was right and its instrument could not have shown it, since the fire-vs-lane gap is denominated in wall time while its arm measured redness on a box at loadavg 18.14); **the BACKGROUND QoS CLASS** (`taskpolicy -c background` = **0.51×**, i.e. 7× worse than where the fire sits, so the fire is nowhere near that tier); and **an inherited background designation** (`taskpolicy -B` on self, then re-measure: **3.64× → 3.76×**, nothing to remove). ⓘ **Reported and untouched: F-1267-2 did NOT reproduce** — the `:42` nearest-actor assertion (2/24 in s1267) is **0 misses in 72 instances** here. ⓘ **Honest note against my own design:** each cycle ran 1→2→3→6, so the middle steps always executed on a box my own earlier arms had heated. ✅ **F-1280-1 (s1280, FOUND + FOLDED INTO THE MASTER — THE HANDOFF'S EDIT PLAN NAMED THE ADVERTISEMENT AND MISSED THE ENFORCER).** s1279 §I.1 specified the rung-conformance scope as *"`auto_pan` 3→2, `place_building` 3 declared, the spec ladder line amended"* and named exactly one code file, `src/agent/ToolSurface.ts`. Re-read at source s1280, **three corrections, each measured**: ⑴ **`auto_pan`'s rung is declared TWICE.** `src/agent/AgentConsent.ts:15` carries `{ id: 'auto_pan', level: 3, … }`, and *that* is the table the consent gate reads — `AgentConsent.allows():63` is `if (!def || def.level > ceiling) return false`, and `ability():94` computes `earned = def.level <= ceiling`. `ToolSurface.implementedCapabilities` is the parallel list feeding `snapshot.agent.capabilities`. **Editing only ToolSurface would have moved the sign and left the gate at rung 3** — the catalog's "cured defect survives in the sibling script" shape, one commit from happening. ⑵ **`place_building` cannot be declared at all yet**: there is no `place_building` member in the `AgentAbility` union (`AgentConsent.ts:3`) and no branch for it in `implementedCapabilities`; adding one means extending a type consumed by `LockstepClient.ts`, `RunSuspend.ts` and `Game.ts` **for an adapter method that does not exist on main**. The owner's own sentence tied it to the branch merge (*"merge save/ap-06b-adapter-wiring … with the derived-list levels adjusted"*), so it is that task's scope. ⑶ ⚠️ **THE WHOLE CHANGE IS INERT ON MAIN, AND NOBODY HAD SAID SO.** `implementedCapabilities` only emits `auto_pan` under `if (typeof game.panAt === 'function')`, and main's agent adapter (`src/game/Game.ts:2062-2064`) wires exactly `repair`, `collectXp`, `collectGold` — **`grep -c panAt src/game/Game.ts` = 0**. `panAt` arrives with `save/ap-06b-adapter-wiring`. So `ToolSurface.ts:378`'s `level: 3` is presently **unreachable code**, no panel row can move, and the two `auto_pan` panel assertions (`m4-09:107`, `m4-10:110`) are `toHaveCount(0)` and hold at either rung. A fire that expected a player-visible diff here would have hunted a regression that cannot exist; one that shipped it as player news would have been wrong. **All three are pre-registered in `tasks/lane-c-agent-rung-conformance.md`** — ⑴ as scope 1+2, ⑵ as an explicit non-goal with its reason, ⑶ as a scope-0 abort (`grep -c panAt` must be **0**, else the premise moved). 💡 **THE CLASS:** *a handoff's §I is a hypothesis, and s1279 said so itself.* The specific trap is that **the file a ruling is easiest to find in is not always the file that enforces it** — `grep` ranks by appearance, not by authority. Before editing a declared constant, ask **who reads it**, not just where it is written.
✅ **F-1281-1 (s1281, MEASURED + CORRECTS s1280 — "WHOLLY INERT ON MAIN" WAS DRAWN FROM ONE OF TWO CONSUMERS, AND THE OTHER ONE CHANGES BEHAVIOUR).** F-1280-1 ⑶ concluded *"THE WHOLE CHANGE IS INERT ON MAIN"* from a true premise: `ToolSurface.implementedCapabilities` emits `auto_pan` only under `if (typeof game.panAt === 'function')`, and `grep -c panAt src/game/Game.ts` = **0** — ✓ re-verified s1281, still 0. **But `AgentConsent` is a second consumer and it is not adapter-gated.** `src/agent/StandingOrders.ts:415-418` maps the `HARVEST` verb to the `auto_pan` ability and `:408-413` refuses an order whose ability is not `allowed`; `allowed = earned && rungs[def.level] && granted` with `earned = def.level <= ceiling` (`AgentConsent.ts:88-97`). 🔬 **Measured, not reasoned** (`logs/session-scratch/s1281/inertness-probe.mjs`, everything else held granted, the declared rung the only variable): at ceiling **1** `allowed=false → false`; at ceiling **3** `true → true`; at ceiling **2** `allowed=false → **true**`. ➡️ **So a level-2 Prospector can now be given a HARVEST standing order and previously could not** — exactly what the owner ordered, arriving on the path nobody had traced. `StandingOrders` is live via `src/agent/Embodiment.ts:11,127` (the embodied agent ticks orders) and `ToolSurface.ts:195` (submission). No duplicate normalized keys exist (227/227 distinct), so "exact" is unambiguous here. Both refusal arms now disclose the other matches, so a shadowed sibling is never invisible. 🧪 **4 NEW ARMS, EACH WITH A CONTROL, MUTATION-PROVED ONE MUTATION PER BRANCH** (subject restored byte-identical, sha256 `28c31df1981d1650`): **M1** revert to pure longest-wins → **3 red** (both exact-match arms + the rider) while the *"no exact match → longest still wins"* arm stayed **GREEN**, which is the control proving the tie-break was **preserved, not replaced** · **M2** drop the blocked-sibling disclosure → **1 red** · **M3** drop the CLOSED arm's `also matched` line → **1 red**. This fire is the fourth turn of the same screw, and the subject was the previous fire's own cure.** Two lessons worth keeping. **(a) A guard that refuses without being able to say WHY does not merely inconvenience the next fire — it manufactures false beliefs, and I can name the cost: an escalation about the owner's money, written by a careful fire, caused by a printed sentence.** **(b) When you re-derive someone's measurement, run THEIR instrument, not your model of it.** My reimplementation of a 12-line function was already wrong by 40% (5 vs 3) because it did not know about a branch seven lines below the one I was reading.* — one rider left open, UNVERIFIED and harmless: `lane-calibrate-suite-workers.md` resolves to the **blocked v2** leaf rather than to its own superseded leaf (the matcher's longest-substring tie-break shadows it), so that leaf is unreachable by its own taskFile. rc=1 either way and the stricter refusal wins, so it is a curiosity, not a hole.

Since `indexOf(x) >= 0` ⟺ `includes(x)`, the throw **guarantees** membership and `mirrored` can never be `true` **for any contract whatsoever** — verified by executing the derivation over all 8 directions (`any mirrored=true reachable? false`). ⚖️ **Not blocking, and deliberately so:** the *outcome* is correct for this contract (the walk8 sheet has explicit `e` **and** `w` rows, so nothing mirrors), all 8 `false` values match the runtime, and s1148's bar clause 4 is genuinely met; `expect(snapshot.mirrored).toBe(false)` still meaningfully asserts the runtime never mirrors. **But the report claims "`mirrored` is false whenever the resolved direction has its own row", implying a live alternative branch, and there is none.** If the contract ever regresses toward the pre-walk8 3-row shape (`s`/`e`/`n`, no `w` — precisely the sheet this slice replaced), the spec **throws at collection** with "no row for nw (w)" instead of expecting a mirror: *the one scenario the column exists to cover is the scenario the code cannot express.* Honest cures: derive `mirrored` from whether the alias crossed the mirror axis, or drop the column and assert `false` outright. ⛔ **Do not "fix" this by widening anything.** e2e-only, fire-authorable.

Its prescription is now the code: `:57` reads `Math.max(1, banditWalk8.frameCount ?? banditWalk8.grid.cols ?? 1)`.** (REAL but minor, s1149 — TEST AND RUNTIME COUNT FRAMES FROM DIFFERENT CONTRACT KEYS.)**~~ The spec enumerates its expected frame set from `grid.cols`; the runtime builds its file list from `Math.max(1, sheet.frameCount ?? grid.cols ?? 1)` (`src/assets/SpriteAnimator.ts:1029`). Today `frameCount = 8 = cols`, so they agree and the test is sound. Should any future contract set `frameCount ≠ cols`, expected and actual diverge and produce a red that **looks like a runtime bug but is a contract-shape disagreement** — an expensive misdiagnosis for a one-word fix. Cheap hardening: derive the test's length from `frameCount ?? cols`, mirroring the runtime. e2e-only, fire-authorable; pairs naturally with F-1149-1.

- ✅ **F-1046-1 (opened AND FIXED s1046) — the Retention Law's tracked mirror was archiving fossils, and the trap has now caught two fires.** ✓ VERIFIED at `scripts/dashboard-gen.sh:73-76`: the mirror loop was `if not os.path.exists(_d): shutil.copy2(_p,_d)`.
